What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi. Well, I guess what brings me in is... I just keep getting stuck on how my jaw looks. I mean, I still get through my work, but... I've noticed I've been slower getting out the door for client meetings lately. And during some of those appointments, I'm just less present, you know? I'm not just imagining it. If you look at the photos from that wedding, it's pretty obvious that the left side is sagging more than the right. So, yeah, I believe it's actually uneven.\n\nDoctor: Have you considered seeking a professional opinion, such as from a plastic surgeon or dermatologist, about the appearance of your jawline?\n\nPatient: No, not really. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi. Well, I guess what brings me in is... I just keep getting stuck on how my jaw looks. There's this sagging, uneven look around my jawline and on one side of my face. I really started focusing on it after seeing myself in a bunch of recent family photos, and now it's just hard to unsee. \n\nI mean, my wife says she barely notices anything at all, and my clients at work don't seem to see it either, but... I just can't seem to stop fixating on it.\n\nDoctor: Is your concern about your jawline affecting your daily life or relationships in any way?\n\nPatient: Yeah, a little bit. I mean, I still get through my work, but... I've noticed I've been slower getting out the door for client meetings lately. And during some of those appointments, I'm just less present, you know? I get distracted thinking about how my face looks to them instead of focusing on their finances. \n\nOutside of work, I've actually skipped a few art openings my wife and I usually go to. I just really didn't want to be seen, or risk being photographed again.\n\nDoctor: How much distress would you say this concern about your jawline is causing you?\n\nPatient: It's definitely bothering me, but... I wouldn't say it's ruining my life or anything. It's more of a constant annoyance. Like, it's frustrating enough that it distracts me and makes me want to avoid certain things, but I'm not, you know, completely falling apart over it. It's just this persistent, uncomfortable feeling that I can't seem to shake.\n\nDoctor: Is your concern only about your jawline or do you also worry about other parts of your body?\n\nPatient: No, it's really just my jawline. I mean... I guess I have normal things, like I wouldn't mind being in slightly better shape, but I don't fixate on any of that. It's strictly just the sagging on that one side of my face. That's the only part that really bothers me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your jawline actually looks uneven and saggy, or is it just that you feel like it does?\n\nPatient: I mean, I think it really does look that way. I'm not just imagining it. If you look at the photos from that wedding, it's pretty obvious that the left side is sagging more than the right. So, yeah, I believe it's actually uneven.\n\nDoctor: Have you considered seeking a professional opinion, such as from a plastic surgeon or dermatologist, about the appearance of your jawline?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean... I haven't looked into seeing a plastic surgeon or anything like that. Like I said, it bothers me, but I don't think I'm at the point where I'd actually go get a procedure done or spend money on a specialist. So, no, I haven't really considered taking it that far.\n\nDoctor: Do you think others would notice the unevenness of your jawline if you didn't point it out to them?\n\nPatient: Well, honestly... I don't think they do notice it, at least not right away. That's actually why I'm here. I keep getting stuck on this sagging, uneven look around my jaw, especially after seeing myself in all those family photos. But my wife tells me she doesn't see what I'm talking about, and my clients... well, they say they barely notice anything different about me. So I know they aren't seeing it the way I am, but I just can't stop focusing on it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your perception of your jawline's appearance is accurate, or could it be exaggerated?\n\nPatient: I mean... that's the confusing part. When I look at those pictures, it feels completely accurate to me. I see the sagging right there. But... I guess logically, since my wife and my clients aren't seeing it, maybe I am blowing it out of proportion a bit? \n\nI don't really know for sure. It just feels very real and obvious to me when I'm looking at it, even if I might be exaggerating it in my own head.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that the sagging and unevenness you see in your jawline might not be as noticeable or present as you think it is?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think it's definitely possible. I mean... if my wife and the people I work with aren't seeing it, then it's probably not as noticeable to the rest of the world as it is to me. It's just hard to shake the feeling when I look in the mirror or see a photo. But logically... yes, I have to admit it might not be as bad as I'm making it out to be.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your relationships or work performance since you started worrying about your jawline?\n\nPatient: Well, I still get through my work for the most part. But... I have noticed I'm slower getting out the door for client meetings lately. I just take longer getting ready, checking the mirror. I guess I've skipped a few art openings recently. I usually enjoy going to them, but I just really didn't want to be seen, or, you know, risk having anyone taking photographs of me. So it is having an impact, yeah.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any chance that the sagging and unevenness you see in your jawline isn't actually there at all?\n\nPatient: I don't know if I'd go that far. I mean, I'm getting older, so there's bound to be some changes, right? I can't imagine I'm just making the whole thing up out of nowhere. I'm sure there's *something* there... I just might be making a bigger deal out of it than it actually is.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. It's just hard to shake the feeling when I look in the mirror or see a photo. But logically... yes, I have to admit it might not be as bad as I'm making it out to be.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your relationships or work performance since you started worrying about your jawline?\n\nPatient: Well, I still get through my work for the most part. But... I have noticed I'm slower getting out the door for client meetings lately. I just take longer getting ready, checking the mirror. And during some of those appointments, I'm probably a bit less present than I should be. My mind just kind of wanders back to how my face looks. \n\nOutside of work... I guess I've skipped a few art openings recently. I usually enjoy going to them, but I just really didn't want to be seen, or, you know, risk having anyone taking photographs of me. So it is having an impact, yeah.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any chance that the sagging and unevenness you see in your jawline isn't actually there at all?\n\nPatient: I don't know if I'd go that far. I just might be making a bigger deal out of it than it actually is.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concern about your jawline would decrease if others confirmed that it looks uneven and saggy?\n\nPatient: I really don't think so. That's the only part that really bothers me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your jawline actually looks uneven and saggy, or is it just that you feel like it does?\n\nPatient: I mean, I think it really does look that way. I'm not just imagining it. If you look at the photos from that wedding, it's pretty obvious that the left side is sagging more than the right. So, yeah, I believe it's actually uneven.\n\nDoctor: Have you considered seeking a professional opinion, such as from a plastic surgeon or dermatologist, about the appearance of your jawline?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean... I haven't looked into seeing a plastic surgeon or anything like that. Like I said, it bothers me, but I don't think I'm at the point where I'd actually go get a procedure done or spend money on a specialist. So, no, I haven't really considered taking it that far.\n\nDoctor: Do you think others would notice the unevenness of your jawline if you didn't point it out to them?\n\nPatient: Well, honestly... I don't think they do notice it, at least not right away. That's actually why I'm here. I keep getting stuck on this sagging, uneven look around my jaw, especially after seeing myself in all those family photos. But my wife tells me she doesn't see what I'm talking about, and my clients... well, they say they barely notice anything different about me. So I know they aren't seeing it the way I am, but I just can't stop focusing on it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your perception of your jawline's appearance is accurate, or could it be exaggerated?\n\nPatient: I mean... that's the confusing part. When I look at those pictures, it feels completely accurate to me. I see the sagging right there. But... I guess logically, since my wife and my clients aren't seeing it, maybe I am blowing it out of proportion a bit? \n\nI don't really know for sure. It just feels very real and obvious to me when I'm looking at it, even if I might be exaggerating it in my own head.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that the sagging and unevenness you see in your jawline might not be as noticeable or present as you think it is?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think it's definitely possible. I mean... if my wife and the people I work with aren't seeing it, then it's probably not as noticeable to the rest of the world as it is to me. It's just hard to shake the feeling when I look in the mirror or see a photo. But logically... yes, I have to admit it might not be as bad as I'm making it out to be.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your relationships or work performance since you started worrying about your jawline?\n\nPatient: Well, I still get through my work for the most part. But... I have noticed I'm slower getting out the door for client meetings lately. I just take longer getting ready, checking the mirror. And during some of those appointments, I'm probably a bit less present than I should be. My mind just kind of wanders back to how my face looks. \n\nOutside of work... I guess I've skipped a few art openings recently. I usually enjoy going to them, but I just really didn't want to be seen, or, you know, risk having anyone taking photographs of me. So it is having an impact, yeah.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any chance that the sagging and unevenness you see in your jawline isn't actually there at all?\n\nPatient: I don't know if I'd go that far. I mean, I'm getting older, so there's bound to be some changes, right? I can't imagine I'm just making the whole thing up out of nowhere. I'm sure there's *something* there... I just might be making a bigger deal out of it than it actually is.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concern about your jawline would decrease if others confirmed that it looks uneven and saggy?\n\nPatient: I really don't think so. If anything... if someone actually came out and told me they noticed it, I think it would just make me feel worse. Because then it would mean it's not just me making a big deal out of it, you know? It would mean everyone else is definitely seeing it too. So, no, I don't think having someone confirm it would make me less concerned. It would probably just make me focus on it even more.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concern about your jawline would change if a doctor or an expert told you that there's nothing medically wrong with it?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure if that would change anything for me. I mean, I know it's not a medical issue like a disease or something. It's just... how it looks. So, a doctor telling me it's medically fine wouldn't really fix the fact that I can see the sagging and the unevenness. It's an appearance thing, not a health thing, if that makes sense.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

I mean, I still get through my work, but... I've noticed I've been slower getting out the door for client meetings lately. And during some of those appointments, I'm just less present, you know? I'm not just imagining it. If you look at the photos from that wedding, it's pretty obvious that the left side is sagging more than the right. So, yeah, I believe it's actually uneven.\n\nDoctor: Have you considered seeking a professional opinion, such as from a plastic surgeon or dermatologist, about the appearance of your jawline?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean... I haven't looked into seeing a plastic surgeon or anything like that. Like I said, it bothers me, but I don't think I'm at the point where I'd actually go get a procedure done or spend money on a specialist. So, no, I haven't really considered taking it that far.\n\nDoctor: Do you think others would notice the unevenness of your jawline if you didn't point it out to them?\n\nPatient: Well, honestly... I don't think they do notice it, at least not right away. That's actually why I'm here. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi. Well, I guess what brings me in is... I just keep getting stuck on how my jaw looks. There's this sagging, uneven look around my jawline and on one side of my face. I really started focusing on it after seeing myself in a bunch of recent family photos, and now it's just hard to unsee. \n\nI mean, my wife says she barely notices anything at all, and my clients at work don't seem to see it either, but... I just can't seem to stop fixating on it.\n\nDoctor: Is your concern about your jawline affecting your daily life or relationships in any way?\n\nPatient: Yeah, a little bit. I mean, I still get through my work, but... I've noticed I've been slower getting out the door for client meetings lately. And during some of those appointments, I'm just less present, you know? I get distracted thinking about how my face looks to them instead of focusing on their finances. \n\nOutside of work, I've actually skipped a few art openings my wife and I usually go to. I just really didn't want to be seen, or risk being photographed again.\n\nDoctor: How much distress would you say this concern about your jawline is causing you?\n\nPatient: It's definitely bothering me, but... I wouldn't say it's ruining my life or anything. It's more of a constant annoyance. Like, it's frustrating enough that it distracts me and makes me want to avoid certain things, but I'm not, you know, completely falling apart over it. It's just this persistent, uncomfortable feeling that I can't seem to shake.\n\nDoctor: Is your concern only about your jawline or do you also worry about other parts of your body?\n\nPatient: No, it's really just my jawline. I mean... I guess I have normal things, like I wouldn't mind being in slightly better shape, but I don't fixate on any of that. It's strictly just the sagging on that one side of my face. That's the only part that really bothers me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your jawline actually looks uneven and saggy, or is it just that you feel like it does?\n\nPatient: I mean, I think it really does look that way. I'm not just imagining it. If you look at the photos from that wedding, it's pretty obvious that the left side is sagging more than the right. So, yeah, I believe it's actually uneven.\n\nDoctor: Have you considered seeking a professional opinion, such as from a plastic surgeon or dermatologist, about the appearance of your jawline?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean... I haven't looked into seeing a plastic surgeon or anything like that. Like I said, it bothers me, but I don't think I'm at the point where I'd actually go get a procedure done or spend money on a specialist. So, no, I haven't really considered taking it that far.\n\nDoctor: Do you think others would notice the unevenness of your jawline if you didn't point it out to them?\n\nPatient: Well, honestly... I don't think they do notice it, at least not right away. That's actually why I'm here. I keep getting stuck on this sagging, uneven look around my jaw, especially after seeing myself in all those family photos. But my wife tells me she doesn't see what I'm talking about, and my clients... well, they say they barely notice anything different about me. So I know they aren't seeing it the way I am, but I just can't stop focusing on it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your perception of your jawline's appearance is accurate, or could it be exaggerated?\n\nPatient: I mean... that's the confusing part. When I look at those pictures, it feels completely accurate to me. I see the sagging right there. But... I guess logically, since my wife and my clients aren't seeing it, maybe I am blowing it out of proportion a bit? \n\nI don't really know for sure. It just feels very real and obvious to me when I'm looking at it, even if I might be exaggerating it in my own head.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that the sagging and unevenness you see in your jawline might not be as noticeable or present as you think it is?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think it's definitely possible. I mean... if my wife and the people I work with aren't seeing it, then it's probably not as noticeable to the rest of the world as it is to me. It's just hard to shake the feeling when I look in the mirror or see a photo. But logically... yes, I have to admit it might not be as bad as I'm making it out to be.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your relationships or work performance since you started worrying about your jawline?\n\nPatient: Well, I still get through my work for the most part. But... I have noticed I'm slower getting out the door for client meetings lately. I just take longer getting ready, checking the mirror. And during some of those appointments, I'm probably a bit less present than I should be. My mind just kind of wanders back to how my face looks. \n\nOutside of work... I guess I've skipped a few art openings recently. I usually enjoy going to them, but I just really didn't want to be seen, or, you know, risk having anyone taking photographs of me. So it is having an impact, yeah.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any chance that the sagging and unevenness you see in your jawline isn't actually there at all?\n\nPatient: I don't know if I'd go that far. I mean, I'm getting older, so there's bound to be some changes, right? I can't imagine I'm just making the whole thing up out of nowhere. I'm sure there's *something* there... I just might be making a bigger deal out of it than it actually is.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concern about your jawline would decrease if others confirmed that it looks uneven and saggy?\n\nPatient: I really don't think so. If anything... if someone actually came out and told me they noticed it, I think it would just make me feel worse. Because then it would mean it's not just me making a big deal out of it, you know? It would mean everyone else is definitely seeing it too. So, no, I don't think having someone confirm it would make me less concerned. It would probably just make me focus on it even more.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concern about your jawline would change if a doctor or an expert told you that there's nothing medically wrong with it?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure if that would change anything for me. So, yeah, I really don't know if taking away mirrors would actually lessen the concern, or if it would just make me stress about it in a different way.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your jawline looks the way you think it does when you're not looking at yourself in a mirror or photos?\n\nPatient: I mean, yeah, I assume it does. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n17\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF45.2, F42.2\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi. Well, I guess what brings me in is... I just keep getting stuck on how my jaw looks. There's this sagging, uneven look around my jawline and on one side of my face. I really started focusing on it after seeing myself in a bunch of recent family photos, and now it's just hard to unsee. \n\nI mean, my wife says she barely notices anything at all, and my clients at work don't seem to see it either, but... I just can't seem to stop fixating on it.\n\nDoctor: Is your concern about your jawline affecting your daily life or relationships in any way?\n\nPatient: Yeah, a little bit. I mean, I still get through my work, but... I've noticed I've been slower getting out the door for client meetings lately. And during some of those appointments, I'm just less present, you know? I get distracted thinking about how my face looks to them instead of focusing on their finances. \n\nOutside of work, I've actually skipped a few art openings my wife and I usually go to. I just really didn't want to be seen, or risk being photographed again.\n\nDoctor: How much distress would you say this concern about your jawline is causing you?\n\nPatient: It's definitely bothering me, but... I wouldn't say it's ruining my life or anything. It's more of a constant annoyance. Like, it's frustrating enough that it distracts me and makes me want to avoid certain things, but I'm not, you know, completely falling apart over it. It's just this persistent, uncomfortable feeling that I can't seem to shake.\n\nDoctor: Is your concern only about your jawline or do you also worry about other parts of your body?\n\nPatient: No, it's really just my jawline. I mean... I guess I have normal things, like I wouldn't mind being in slightly better shape, but I don't fixate on any of that. It's strictly just the sagging on that one side of my face. That's the only part that really bothers me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your jawline actually looks uneven and saggy, or is it just that you feel like it does?\n\nPatient: I mean, I think it really does look that way. I'm not just imagining it. If you look at the photos from that wedding, it's pretty obvious that the left side is sagging more than the right. So, yeah, I believe it's actually uneven.\n\nDoctor: Have you considered seeking a professional opinion, such as from a plastic surgeon or dermatologist, about the appearance of your jawline?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean... I haven't looked into seeing a plastic surgeon or anything like that. Like I said, it bothers me, but I don't think I'm at the point where I'd actually go get a procedure done or spend money on a specialist. So, no, I haven't really considered taking it that far.\n\nDoctor: Do you think others would notice the unevenness of your jawline if you didn't point it out to them?\n\nPatient: Well, honestly... I don't think they do notice it, at least not right away. That's actually why I'm here. I keep getting stuck on this sagging, uneven look around my jaw, especially after seeing myself in all those family photos. But my wife tells me she doesn't see what I'm talking about, and my clients... well, they say they barely notice anything different about me. So I know they aren't seeing it the way I am, but I just can't stop focusing on it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your perception of your jawline's appearance is accurate, or could it be exaggerated?\n\nPatient: I mean... that's the confusing part. When I look at those pictures, it feels completely accurate to me. I see the sagging right there. But... I guess logically, since my wife and my clients aren't seeing it, maybe I am blowing it out of proportion a bit? \n\nI don't really know for sure. It just feels very real and obvious to me when I'm looking at it, even if I might be exaggerating it in my own head.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that the sagging and unevenness you see in your jawline might not be as noticeable or present as you think it is?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think it's definitely possible. I mean... if my wife and the people I work with aren't seeing it, then it's probably not as noticeable to the rest of the world as it is to me. It's just hard to shake the feeling when I look in the mirror or see a photo. But logically... yes, I have to admit it might not be as bad as I'm making it out to be.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your relationships or work performance since you started worrying about your jawline?\n\nPatient: Well, I still get through my work for the most part. But... I have noticed I'm slower getting out the door for client meetings lately. I just take longer getting ready, checking the mirror. And during some of those appointments, I'm probably a bit less present than I should be. My mind just kind of wanders back to how my face looks. \n\nOutside of work... I guess I've skipped a few art openings recently. I usually enjoy going to them, but I just really didn't want to be seen, or, you know, risk having anyone taking photographs of me. So it is having an impact, yeah.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any chance that the sagging and unevenness you see in your jawline isn't actually there at all?\n\nPatient: I don't know if I'd go that far. I mean, I'm getting older, so there's bound to be some changes, right? I can't imagine I'm just making the whole thing up out of nowhere. I'm sure there's *something* there... I just might be making a bigger deal out of it than it actually is.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concern about your jawline would decrease if others confirmed that it looks uneven and saggy?\n\nPatient: I really don't think so. If anything... if someone actually came out and told me they noticed it, I think it would just make me feel worse. Because then it would mean it's not just me making a big deal out of it, you know? It would mean everyone else is definitely seeing it too. So, no, I don't think having someone confirm it would make me less concerned. It would probably just make me focus on it even more.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concern about your jawline would change if a doctor or an expert told you that there's nothing medically wrong with it?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure if that would change anything for me. I mean, I know it's not a medical issue like a disease or something. It's just... how it looks. It's an appearance thing, not a health thing, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Is your concern about your jawline related to a specific feature or aspect of it that you believe is flawed?\n\nPatient: Yeah, exactly. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don't know... maybe? I guess if I saw a picture where it was fixed, I might feel a little better for a minute. But honestly, I think it might just make me more aware of how bad the real photos look in comparison. 

Like, if I saw what I *could* look like, it might just remind me that my actual jawline doesn't look like that. So I'm not sure it would really fix the concern. It might just make me want to actually change it more.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n18\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF45.2, F42.2\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi. Well, I guess what brings me in is... I just keep getting stuck on how my jaw looks. There's this sagging, uneven look around my jawline and on one side of my face. I really started focusing on it after seeing myself in a bunch of recent family photos, and now it's just hard to unsee. \n\nI mean, my wife says she barely notices anything at all, and my clients at work don't seem to see it either, but... I just can't seem to stop fixating on it.\n\nDoctor: Is your concern about your jawline affecting your daily life or relationships in any way?\n\nPatient: Yeah, a little bit. I mean, I still get through my work, but... I've noticed I've been slower getting out the door for client meetings lately. And during some of those appointments, I'm just less present, you know? I get distracted thinking about how my face looks to them instead of focusing on their finances. \n\nOutside of work, I've actually skipped a few art openings my wife and I usually go to. I just really didn't want to be seen, or risk being photographed again.\n\nDoctor: How much distress would you say this concern about your jawline is causing you?\n\nPatient: It's definitely bothering me, but... I wouldn't say it's ruining my life or anything. It's more of a constant annoyance. Like, it's frustrating enough that it distracts me and makes me want to avoid certain things, but I'm not, you know, completely falling apart over it. It's just this persistent, uncomfortable feeling that I can't seem to shake.\n\nDoctor: Is your concern only about your jawline or do you also worry about other parts of your body?\n\nPatient: No, it's really just my jawline. I mean... I guess I have normal things, like I wouldn't mind being in slightly better shape, but I don't fixate on any of that. It's strictly just the sagging on that one side of my face. That's the only part that really bothers me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your jawline actually looks uneven and saggy, or is it just that you feel like it does?\n\nPatient: I mean, I think it really does look that way. I'm not just imagining it. If you look at the photos from that wedding, it's pretty obvious that the left side is sagging more than the right. So, yeah, I believe it's actually uneven.\n\nDoctor: Have you considered seeking a professional opinion, such as from a plastic surgeon or dermatologist, about the appearance of your jawline?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean... I haven't looked into seeing a plastic surgeon or anything like that. Like I said, it bothers me, but I don't think I'm at the point where I'd actually go get a procedure done or spend money on a specialist. So, no, I haven't really considered taking it that far.\n\nDoctor: Do you think others would notice the unevenness of your jawline if you didn't point it out to them?\n\nPatient: Well, honestly... I don't think they do notice it, at least not right away. That's actually why I'm here. I keep getting stuck on this sagging, uneven look around my jaw, especially after seeing myself in all those family photos. But my wife tells me she doesn't see what I'm talking about, and my clients... well, they say they barely notice anything different about me. So I know they aren't seeing it the way I am, but I just can't stop focusing on it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your perception of your jawline's appearance is accurate, or could it be exaggerated?\n\nPatient: I mean... that's the confusing part. When I look at those pictures, it feels completely accurate to me. I see the sagging right there. But... I guess logically, since my wife and my clients aren't seeing it, maybe I am blowing it out of proportion a bit? \n\nI don't really know for sure. It just feels very real and obvious to me when I'm looking at it, even if I might be exaggerating it in my own head.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that the sagging and unevenness you see in your jawline might not be as noticeable or present as you think it is?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think it's definitely possible. I mean... if my wife and the people I work with aren't seeing it, then it's probably not as noticeable to the rest of the world as it is to me. It's just hard to shake the feeling when I look in the mirror or see a photo. But logically... yes, I have to admit it might not be as bad as I'm making it out to be.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your relationships or work performance since you started worrying about your jawline?\n\nPatient: Well, I still get through my work for the most part. But... I have noticed I'm slower getting out the door for client meetings lately. I just take longer getting ready, checking the mirror. And during some of those appointments, I'm probably a bit less present than I should be. My mind just kind of wanders back to how my face looks. \n\nOutside of work... I guess I've skipped a few art openings recently. I usually enjoy going to them, but I just really didn't want to be seen, or, you know, risk having anyone taking photographs of me. So it is having an impact, yeah.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any chance that the sagging and unevenness you see in your jawline isn't actually there at all?\n\nPatient: I don't know if I'd go that far. I mean, I'm getting older, so there's bound to be some changes, right? I can't imagine I'm just making the whole thing up out of nowhere. I'm sure there's *something* there... I just might be making a bigger deal out of it than it actually is.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concern about your jawline would decrease if others confirmed that it looks uneven and saggy?\n\nPatient: I really don't think so. If anything... if someone actually came out and told me they noticed it, I think it would just make me feel worse. Because then it would mean it's not just me making a big deal out of it, you know? It would mean everyone else is definitely seeing it too. So, no, I don't think having someone confirm it would make me less concerned. It would probably just make me focus on it even more.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concern about your jawline would change if a doctor or an expert told you that there's nothing medically wrong with it?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure if that would change anything for me. I mean, I know it's not a medical issue like a disease or something. It's just... how it looks. So, a doctor telling me it's medically fine wouldn't really fix the fact that I can see the sagging and the unevenness. It's an appearance thing, not a health thing, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Is your concern about your jawline related to a specific feature or aspect of it that you believe is flawed?\n\nPatient: Yeah, exactly. It's really just that specific area. I keep getting stuck on this sagging, uneven look right around my jawline and on one side of my face. That's actually what brings me in to see you. We took all these family photos recently, and seeing myself in those... I just couldn't unsee it. I mean, my wife says she barely notices anything, and my clients at work haven't acted like they see it either, but... yeah, that sagging and unevenness is the specific thing I'm focused on.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concern about your jawline would lessen if you couldn't see yourself in mirrors or photos for a while?\n\nPatient: I'm honestly not sure. I haven't really tried doing that. I guess... maybe? I mean, seeing those family photos is what really got me focused on it lately, so maybe not seeing them would help. \n\nBut then again, if I couldn't check a mirror at all, I might just sit there wondering how bad it looks or worrying about what other people are seeing. So, yeah, I really don't know if taking away mirrors would actually lessen the concern, or if it would just make me stress about it in a different way.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your jawline looks the way you think it does when you're not looking at yourself in a mirror or photos?\n\nPatient: I mean, yeah, I assume it does. I don't think my face physically changes just because I step away from a mirror. \n\nIt's... well, when I'm not looking at it, I just know it's there based on what I saw in those photos or in the bathroom that morning. So, yes, I believe the sagging is still there while I'm just sitting at my desk or going about my day. I don't think it goes away when I'm not looking, if that's what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concern about your jawline would decrease if you were certain that no one else could see the sagging and unevenness you perceive?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, to be honest. I mean... like I mentioned, my wife already tells me she doesn't really see it, and my clients don't seem to notice either. But it still bothers me. \n\nSo, even if I was one hundred percent sure no one else could see it... I think I'd still be focused on it. Because I know it's there, you know? It's more about what I see when I look at those pictures of myself. So, I don't really think my concern would decrease all that much.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concern about your jawline would change if you were shown a photo of yourself where the sagging and unevenness you perceive had been digitally removed?\n\nPatient: I don't know... maybe? I guess if I saw a picture where it was fixed, I might feel a little better for a minute. But honestly, I think it might just make me more aware of how bad the real photos look in comparison. \n\nLike, if I saw what I *could* look like, it might just remind me that my actual jawline doesn't look like that. So I'm not sure it would really fix the concern. It might just make me want to actually change it more.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi. Well, I guess what brings me in is... I just keep getting stuck on how my jaw looks. There's this sagging, uneven look around my jawline and on one side of my face. I really started focusing on it after seeing myself in a bunch of recent family photos, and now it's just hard to unsee. \n\nI mean, my wife says she barely notices anything at all, and my clients at work don't seem to see it either, but... I just can't seem to stop fixating on it.\n\nDoctor: Is your concern about your jawline affecting your daily life or relationships in any way?\n\nPatient: Yeah, a little bit. I mean, I still get through my work, but... I've noticed I've been slower getting out the door for client meetings lately. And during some of those appointments, I'm just less present, you know? I get distracted thinking about how my face looks to them instead of focusing on their finances. \n\nOutside of work, I've actually skipped a few art openings my wife and I usually go to. I just really didn't want to be seen, or risk being photographed again.\n\nDoctor: How much distress would you say this concern about your jawline is causing you?\n\nPatient: It's definitely bothering me, but... I wouldn't say it's ruining my life or anything. It's more of a constant annoyance. Like, it's frustrating enough that it distracts me and makes me want to avoid certain things, but I'm not, you know, completely falling apart over it. It's just this persistent, uncomfortable feeling that I can't seem to shake.\n\nDoctor: Is your concern only about your jawline or do you also worry about other parts of your body?\n\nPatient: No, it's really just my jawline. I mean... I guess I have normal things, like I wouldn't mind being in slightly better shape, but I don't fixate on any of that. It's strictly just the sagging on that one side of my face. That's the only part that really bothers me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your jawline actually looks uneven and saggy, or is it just that you feel like it does?\n\nPatient: I mean, I think it really does look that way. I'm not just imagining it. If you look at the photos from that wedding, it's pretty obvious that the left side is sagging more than the right. So, yeah, I believe it's actually uneven.\n\nDoctor: Have you considered seeking a professional opinion, such as from a plastic surgeon or dermatologist, about the appearance of your jawline?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean... I haven't looked into seeing a plastic surgeon or anything like that. Like I said, it bothers me, but I don't think I'm at the point where I'd actually go get a procedure done or spend money on a specialist. So, no, I haven't really considered taking it that far.\n\nDoctor: Do you think others would notice the unevenness of your jawline if you didn't point it out to them?\n\nPatient: Well, honestly... I don't think they do notice it, at least not right away. That's actually why I'm here. I keep getting stuck on this sagging, uneven look around my jaw, especially after seeing myself in all those family photos. But my wife tells me she doesn't see what I'm talking about, and my clients... well, they say they barely notice anything different about me. So I know they aren't seeing it the way I am, but I just can't stop focusing on it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your perception of your jawline's appearance is accurate, or could it be exaggerated?\n\nPatient: I mean... that's the confusing part. When I look at those pictures, it feels completely accurate to me. I see the sagging right there. But... I guess logically, since my wife and my clients aren't seeing it, maybe I am blowing it out of proportion a bit? \n\nI don't really know for sure. It just feels very real and obvious to me when I'm looking at it, even if I might be exaggerating it in my own head.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that the sagging and unevenness you see in your jawline might not be as noticeable or present as you think it is?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think it's definitely possible. I mean... if my wife and the people I work with aren't seeing it, then it's probably not as noticeable to the rest of the world as it is to me. It's just hard to shake the feeling when I look in the mirror or see a photo. But logically... yes, I have to admit it might not be as bad as I'm making it out to be.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your relationships or work performance since you started worrying about your jawline?\n\nPatient: Well, I still get through my work for the most part. But... I have noticed I'm slower getting out the door for client meetings lately. I just take longer getting ready, checking the mirror. And during some of those appointments, I'm probably a bit less present than I should be. My mind just kind of wanders back to how my face looks. \n\nOutside of work... I guess I've skipped a few art openings recently. I usually enjoy going to them, but I just really didn't want to be seen, or, you know, risk having anyone taking photographs of me. So it is having an impact, yeah.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any chance that the sagging and unevenness you see in your jawline isn't actually there at all?\n\nPatient: I don't know if I'd go that far. I mean, I'm getting older, so there's bound to be some changes, right? I can't imagine I'm just making the whole thing up out of nowhere. I'm sure there's *something* there... I just might be making a bigger deal out of it than it actually is.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concern about your jawline would decrease if others confirmed that it looks uneven and saggy?\n\nPatient: I really don't think so. If anything... if someone actually came out and told me they noticed it, I think it would just make me feel worse. Because then it would mean it's not just me making a big deal out of it, you know? It would mean everyone else is definitely seeing it too. So, no, I don't think having someone confirm it would make me less concerned. It would probably just make me focus on it even more.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concern about your jawline would change if a doctor or an expert told you that there's nothing medically wrong with it?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure if that would change anything for me. I mean, I know it's not a medical issue like a disease or something. It's just... how it looks. So, a doctor telling me it's medically fine wouldn't really fix the fact that I can see the sagging and the unevenness. It's an appearance thing, not a health thing, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Is your concern about your jawline related to a specific feature or aspect of it that you believe is flawed?\n\nPatient: Yeah, exactly. It's really just that specific area. I keep getting stuck on this sagging, uneven look right around my jawline and on one side of my face. That's actually what brings me in to see you. We took all these family photos recently, and seeing myself in those... I just couldn't unsee it. I mean, my wife says she barely notices anything, and my clients at work haven't acted like they see it either, but... yeah, that sagging and unevenness is the specific thing I'm focused on.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concern about your jawline would lessen if you couldn't see yourself in mirrors or photos for a while?\n\nPatient: I'm honestly not sure. I haven't really tried doing that. I guess... maybe? I mean, seeing those family photos is what really got me focused on it lately, so maybe not seeing them would help. \n\nBut then again, if I couldn't check a mirror at all, I might just sit there wondering how bad it looks or worrying about what other people are seeing. So, yeah, I really don't know if taking away mirrors would actually lessen the concern, or if it would just make me stress about it in a different way.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your jawline looks the way you think it does when you're not looking at yourself in a mirror or photos?\n\nPatient: I mean, yeah, I assume it does. I don't think my face physically changes just because I step away from a mirror. \n\nIt's... well, when I'm not looking at it, I just know it's there based on what I saw in those photos or in the bathroom that morning. So, yes, I believe the sagging is still there while I'm just sitting at my desk or going about my day. I don't think it goes away when I'm not looking, if that's what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concern about your jawline would decrease if you were certain that no one else could see the sagging and unevenness you perceive?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, to be honest. I mean... like I mentioned, my wife already tells me she doesn't really see it, and my clients don't seem to notice either. It's more about what I see when I look at those pictures of myself. So, I don't really think my concern would decrease all that much.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concern about your jawline would change if you were shown a photo of yourself where the sagging and unevenness you perceive had been digitally removed?\n\nPatient: I don't know... maybe? I don't think it goes away when I'm not looking, if that's what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concern about your jawline would decrease if you were certain that no one else could see the sagging and unevenness you perceive?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, to be honest. It's more about what I see when I look at those pictures of myself. I just might be making a bigger deal out of it than it actually is.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concern about your jawline would decrease if others confirmed that it looks uneven and saggy?\n\nPatient: I really don't think so. If anything... if someone actually came out and told me they noticed it, I think it would just make me feel worse. Because then it would mean it's not just me making a big deal out of it, you know? It would mean everyone else is definitely seeing it too. So, no, I don't think having someone confirm it would make me less concerned. It would probably just make me focus on it even more.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concern about your jawline would change if a doctor or an expert told you that there's nothing medically wrong with it?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure if that would change anything for me. I haven't really tried doing that. I guess... maybe? So, no, I haven't really considered taking it that far.\n\nDoctor: Do you think others would notice the unevenness of your jawline if you didn't point it out to them?\n\nPatient: Well, honestly... I don't think they do notice it, at least not right away. That's actually why I'm here. I keep getting stuck on this sagging, uneven look around my jaw, especially after seeing myself in all those family photos. But my wife tells me she doesn't see what I'm talking about, and my clients... well, they say they barely notice anything different about me. So I know they aren't seeing it the way I am, but I just can't stop focusing on it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your perception of your jawline's appearance is accurate, or could it be exaggerated?\n\nPatient: I mean... that's the confusing part. When I look at those pictures, it feels completely accurate to me. I see the sagging right there. But... I guess logically, since my wife and my clients aren't seeing it, maybe I am blowing it out of proportion a bit? \n\nI don't really know for sure. It just feels very real and obvious to me when I'm looking at it, even if I might be exaggerating it in my own head.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that the sagging and unevenness you see in your jawline might not be as noticeable or present as you think it is?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think it's definitely possible. I mean... if my wife and the people I work with aren't seeing it, then it's probably not as noticeable to the rest of the world as it is to me. It's just hard to shake the feeling when I look in the mirror or see a photo. But logically... yes, I have to admit it might not be as bad as I'm making it out to be.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your relationships or work performance since you started worrying about your jawline?\n\nPatient: Well, I still get through my work for the most part. But... I have noticed I'm slower getting out the door for client meetings lately. I just take longer getting ready, checking the mirror. And during some of those appointments, I'm probably a bit less present than I should be. My mind just kind of wanders back to how my face looks. \n\nOutside of work... I guess I've skipped a few art openings recently. I usually enjoy going to them, but I just really didn't want to be seen, or, you know, risk having anyone taking photographs of me. So it is having an impact, yeah.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any chance that the sagging and unevenness you see in your jawline isn't actually there at all?\n\nPatient: I don't know if I'd go that far. I mean, I'm getting older, so there's bound to be some changes, right? I can't imagine I'm just making the whole thing up out of nowhere. I'm sure there's *something* there... I just might be making a bigger deal out of it than it actually is.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concern about your jawline would decrease if others confirmed that it looks uneven and saggy?\n\nPatient: I really don't think so. If anything... if someone actually came out and told me they noticed it, I think it would just make me feel worse. Because then it would mean it's not just me making a big deal out of it, you know? It would mean everyone else is definitely seeing it too. So, no, I don't think having someone confirm it would make me less concerned. It would probably just make me focus on it even more.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concern about your jawline would change if a doctor or an expert told you that there's nothing medically wrong with it?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure if that would change anything for me. I mean, I know it's not a medical issue like a disease or something. It's just... how it looks. So, a doctor telling me it's medically fine wouldn't really fix the fact that I can see the sagging and the unevenness. It's an appearance thing, not a health thing, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Is your concern about your jawline related to a specific feature or aspect of it that you believe is flawed?\n\nPatient: Yeah, exactly. It's really just that specific area. I keep getting stuck on this sagging, uneven look right around my jawline and on one side of my face. That's actually what brings me in to see you. We took all these family photos recently, and seeing myself in those... I just couldn't unsee it. I mean, my wife says she barely notices anything, and my clients at work haven't acted like they see it either, but... yeah, that sagging and unevenness is the specific thing I'm focused on.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concern about your jawline would lessen if you couldn't see yourself in mirrors or photos for a while?\n\nPatient: I'm honestly not sure. I haven't really tried doing that. I guess... maybe? I mean, seeing those family photos is what really got me focused on it lately, so maybe not seeing them would help. \n\nBut then again, if I couldn't check a mirror at all, I might just sit there wondering how bad it looks or worrying about what other people are seeing. So, yeah, I really don't know if taking away mirrors would actually lessen the concern, or if it would just make me stress about it in a different way.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your jawline looks the way you think it does when you're not looking at yourself in a mirror or photos?\n\nPatient: I mean, yeah, I assume it does. I don't think my face physically changes just because I step away from a mirror. \n\nIt's... well, when I'm not looking at it, I just know it's there based on what I saw in those photos or in the bathroom that morning. So, yes, I believe the sagging is still there while I'm just sitting at my desk or going about my day. I don't think it goes away when I'm not looking, if that's what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concern about your jawline would decrease if you were certain that no one else could see the sagging and unevenness you perceive?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, to be honest. I mean... like I mentioned, my wife already tells me she doesn't really see it, and my clients don't seem to notice either. But it still bothers me. \n\nSo, even if I was one hundred percent sure no one else could see it... I think I'd still be focused on it. Because I know it's there, you know? It's more about what I see when I look at those pictures of myself. So, I don't really think my concern would decrease all that much.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concern about your jawline would change if you were shown a photo of yourself where the sagging and unevenness you perceive had been digitally removed?\n\nPatient: I don't know... maybe? I guess if I saw a picture where it was fixed, I might feel a little better for a minute. But honestly, I think it might just make me more aware of how bad the real photos look in comparison. \n\nLike, if I saw what I *could* look like, it might just remind me that my actual jawline doesn't look like that. So I'm not sure it would really fix the concern. It might just make me want to actually change it more.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your jawline looks uneven and saggy even when others tell you it doesn't?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. I mean, my wife tells me she doesn't see it, and I know people aren't pointing it out or anything, but... yes, I still think it looks that way. \n\nJust because someone else says they don't notice it doesn't change what I see in the mirror or in those photos. It's right there. So, yeah, even when they tell me it's fine, I still see it as saggy and uneven.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your life would be significantly better if your jawline looked perfectly even and symmetrical?\n\nPatient: I don't know if \"significantly better\" is the right way to put it. I mean... I guess I'd feel a bit more confident, especially when I'm meeting with new clients or looking at photos of myself. It would definitely be one less thing on my mind. \n\nBut my life overall? My marriage is good, my business is doing fine. It's not like my jawline is ruining my whole life or anything. It's just... a constant annoyance that I wish I could fix. So, better, sure. But probably not a total life-changer."
